ETN — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • The FY2025 10-K states that sales recognised over time were approximately 7% of consolidated net sales in 2025 and under 5% in 2024 and 2023, against Systems at $13,000m or 47% of consolidated sales.
  • Six large electrical customers accounted for 22% of the two segments' FY2025 sales, about $4,420m, an average of roughly $740m each; Eaton identifies them only as a group.
  • Backlog counts firm-committed orders only. The 10-K states that a significant portion of open orders are not firm because they have historically been subject to customer release, so the disclosed order book is the committed subset.
  • Content is qualified onto an airframe or engine programme under the type certificate, then sold across a twenty- to thirty-year production run and a longer maintenance tail.
  • Aftermarket passed OEM for the first time in H1 2026 at $920m against $864m and supplied 57% of the half's revenue growth.
  • Customer concentration is the highest in the company: four vehicle OEMs took 37% of FY2025 Vehicle sales and one OEM took 18% of eMobility sales.
  • Eaton hedges almost none of it: its own market-risk disclosure states that a hypothetical 10% move in commodity prices would produce a gain or loss of less than $1 million, against Vertiv's disclosed $15.8m on roughly 37% of Eaton's revenue.
  • Realised operating leverage in Electrical ran 44–50% incremental in FY2023–FY2024, 29% in FY2025 and 12% in H1 2026 excluding Boyd.
  • Electrical Americas has the highest gross margin in the company and converts about 30 cents of every revenue dollar into segment profit; Electrical Global converts about 19 cents.
capital-allocation
  • Free cash flow conversion is the commitment furthest from its target and the one that has been missed. FY2025 free cash flow of $3,553m against adjusted earnings of $4,722m is 75%, and the FY2026 guide of $3.9-4.3bn against roughly $5,250m implies about 78%, against a 95-100% commitment for 2030.
competitive-advantage
  • Return on capital employed excluding goodwill and acquired intangibles has been between 28% and 39% in every year since 2016 except the 2020 divestiture-and-COVID year, and was 38.9% in FY2025 on a company-wide basis.
  • Pricing power failed its own test in the most recent quarter. Eaton's consolidated gross margin declined 350 basis points year over year in Q2 2026, from 37.0% to 33.5%, and Electrical Americas margin declined from 29.5% to 27.5% while organic revenue grew 18%.
  • Eaton's combined Electrical margin declined 172 basis points to 24.5% on 18% organic growth. The Eaton premium over ABB Electrification moved from +330 basis points in FY2024 and +290 in FY2025 to −40 basis points in Q2 2026.
  • Boyd Thermal cost $9,549m net of cash. On its stub economics and Eaton's own $1.8bn full-year revenue figure, Boyd generates roughly $415m of segment operating profit, about 3.3% after tax on the price paid, against a cost of capital estimated at 8–10%
  • Powell Industries, at $1,104m of FY2025 revenue with almost no goodwill, earned a 29.0% return on capital employed, a 19.7% operating margin and a 16.3% five-year revenue CAGR, and its latest-quarter operating margin of 20.6% is above Eaton's consolidated figure.
  • The 2020–2025 margin expansion was cohort-average: Eaton Electrical +790 basis points against Rockwell +860, Hubbell +730 and Parker's total segment margin +740 on their own consolidated results.
  • Placement: this advantage earns excess returns today and is defensible over the next several years, exposed to erosion as customers consolidate their buying, as rivals with equal engineering depth close the portfolio gap, and as the 800-volt DC architecture reopens specification decisions that were settled.
  • a repeatable pattern of winning more content on each successor platform — 2–3x the F-16 on the F-35, 2–3x the KC-135 on the KC-46A, 2x the A330/A340 on the A350, more than 8x the Black Hawk on FLRAA
  • Eaton bought most of this franchise: Souriau-Sunbank $907m in December 2019, Cobham Mission Systems $2,800m in June 2021, Ultra PCS $1,529m in January 2026 — $5,236m of purchase price against a segment earning $1,013m of pre-amortisation operating profit in FY2025, with segment goodwill at $2,977m at 31 December 2025 against $941m at the end of 2018.
  • Aerospace revenue grew from $2,335m in FY2018 to $4,249m in FY2025, up 82%; chaining the organic bridges Eaton discloses each year — +7%, −22%, −2%, +11%, +11%, +10%, +12% — gives about 24% of cumulative organic growth, a 3.1% annual rate, with the rest from Souriau, Cobham and currency.
  • Eaton's Aerospace margin moved 70 basis points in three years, from 23.2% in FY2022 to 23.9% in FY2025, and is 22.8% in the latest quarter.
  • Mobility earns a small excess return on a narrow base. Returns are a few points above a plausible cost of capital, and the part of the business producing the premium is the aftermarket and the commercial-vehicle transmission and clutch installed base, roughly a quarter of revenue.
competitive-landscape
  • Eaton Electrical is the largest single reportable electrical franchise among the US-listed comparables and the second- or third-largest data-centre electrical business globally, behind Schneider Electric and roughly level with Vertiv.
  • Its Electrical revenue compounded 11.4% a year over FY2023–FY2025, roughly half the rate of Vertiv (22.1%), nVent (20.8%) and GE Vernova Electrification (23.0%) and about the same as ABB Electrification.
  • Adding Vertiv at even 70% data-centre gives roughly $33bn across the named western players before Delta, Huawei, Mitsubishi, Siemens and the private modular integrators. On that base Eaton's share is roughly 17–19%, Schneider's roughly 41% and Vertiv's roughly 22%.
  • Both say the same thing about the metric: $3.4m per megawatt is the full bill of materials Eaton can address on a site it wins in full.
  • Eaton has the largest disclosed firm backlog in the group at $18.8bn, ahead of Vertiv's last-disclosed $15.0bn and ABB Electrification's $13.7bn. It does not have the largest data-centre revenue, and it does not have the fastest-growing order book
  • Eaton's whole disclosed utility end market is smaller than Hubbell's Utility Solutions segment and roughly 46% of GE Vernova's Grid Solutions.
  • If the underlying market grows 23% and five players each grow 50–100%, either the 23% is understated or share is moving from unlisted and Asian suppliers.
  • Its Americas segment operating margin was 27.6% in Q2 2026 against Eaton Electrical Americas' 27.5%, having been 24.0% against 29.5% a year earlier, a 560 basis point relative swing in four quarters.
  • Summing each supplier's own disclosed aerospace revenue for its latest fiscal year gives a set of $92,179m, in which Eaton's $4,249m is 4.6%, and 3.5% on the aerospace-only base after removing industrial filtration and golf grips.
  • Eaton's 23.9% excludes every dollar of intangible amortisation, which Corporate holds; company amortisation was $486m in FY2025 and $395m in H1 2026 against $235m in H1 2025.
  • Eaton is not the scale leader in any Mobility business line. Its $3,109m of FY2025 revenue is roughly 40% of Dana's $7,500m of continuing-operations sales and 22% of BorgWarner's $14,316m, and eMobility is about a quarter the size of BorgWarner's PowerDrive Systems, which grew 21% in 2025 while eMobility declined 9%.
  • Three of PACCAR's four named North American truck competitors now sell an in-house automated transmission — Daimler's Detroit DT12, PACCAR's TX-12, Volvo's I-Shift, which took 94% of Volvo Group's own North American orders as of 2021 [web].
critical-points
  • The FY2026 guide of 28.8–29.2% needs H2 near 31.2%, a 450–500 basis point step of which the CFO attributes 300 basis points to price/cost and 150–200 to output and productivity. The comparable 2025 step was about 40 basis points.
  • Data centres accounted for roughly 60% of Electrical's FY2025 revenue increase of $2,407m and commercial and institutional roughly a quarter, together about 85%. Industrial facilities, 15% of Electrical revenue, contributed roughly 1%.
  • The 2030 plan requires Electrical to reach about 30%; if Global holds near 19.5%, Americas has to reach roughly 35%, against its own 32% target and a record annual high of 30.2%.
expectations
  • Across eighteen quarters of numeric organic guidance Eaton landed above the midpoint thirteen times and below the range twice, both in the second half of 2025.
  • Management reaffirmed the 2026 and 2030 commitments on 31 July 2026 and has said it is "too early to provide new targets for 2030", with no investor day planned for 2026.
forward-model
  • Data centres and distributed IT move from 29.0% of Electrical Americas revenue in 2025 to 45.8% in 2030, so the base concentrates the segment.
  • The base delivers the volume and misses the margin on every segment. Reaching the plan needs the acquisitions: base-case surplus capital of $14,633m over 2027–2032 plus $10,600m of modelled buybacks is $25.2bn of discretionary capital against roughly $21bn of stated capacity, and none of it is attributed any revenue here.
m-and-a
  • Boyd Thermal was bought at 22.5x its estimated 2026 adjusted EBITDA, roughly 60% above the top of that band, and is expected to be accretive to adjusted earnings only in year two.
management-claims
  • Mobility's FY2025 EBITDA margin of 17.0% is roughly double Dana's 6.5%, American Axle's 9.8% and BorgWarner's 8.8%, on a revenue base one-half to one-quarter their size. Against Allison Transmission, which converts a comparable commercial-vehicle drivetrain incumbency into a 33.1% EBITDA margin and roughly 21% ROIC, Mobility runs 16 points behind on margin and roughly 9–12 points behind on returns.
secular-trends
  • The bucket went from $4,229m in FY2024 to $5,764m in FY2025, up 36%; Fibrebond closed on 1 April 2025 with about $378m of trailing sales, so roughly $290m of the increase is acquired and organic growth of the bucket was about 29%.
  • The rate declines at every step from announcement toward physical completion. Eaton's revenue is generated nearest the put-in-place step, which grew 46%. Measured there, Eaton's 65% is about 19 points of outgrowth; measured against the 23% market figure management uses on the call, it is 42.
  • The March 2022 investor conference planned Electrical from an $11.1bn 2020 base to $16.5bn by 2025 at an 8.2% CAGR; realised was $20,091m at 12.6%.
  • Eaton's own guidance is consistent with full-year data-centre and distributed-IT growth of roughly 31-37% if the rest of Electrical grows 5-7%, against the 65% quarterly rate management highlights. It is not consistent with 65% data centres and broad-based double-digit growth elsewhere.
  • a bounded range with an explicit architecture caveat, "not every data center is going to require it", became a single point offered as a modelling input.
  • On the observed build rate the 307 GW pipeline is thirty to fifty years of backlog. Two figures management gave on the same call cannot both describe the same quantity.
technology
  • The technology position Eaton presents to investors in 2026 was substantially purchased, and the Boyd purchase-price allocation says so: $7,190m to other intangibles and $3,733m to goodwill against $196m of property, plant and equipment.
  • A solid-state transformer replaces the wound transformer with a power-electronics stage that converts the utility feed straight to DC, so the DC flows to the rack with no further conversions.
  • NVIDIA's 800-volt DC ecosystem post names nine data-centre power-systems partners — ABB, Eaton, GE Vernova, Heron Power, Hitachi Energy, Mitsubishi Electric, Schneider Electric, Siemens and Vertiv
  • Eaton's Electrical CTO puts mass adoption in 2028–2029, so the architecture Eaton claims leadership in becomes dominant largely after the 2030 targets are due.
  • Vertiv's PowerUPS 9000 and Schneider's Galaxy VXL both publish up to 97.5% double-conversion and up to 99% eco-mode efficiency. On published specification the efficiency gap against the two closest listed competitors is zero, and each of the three claims the smallest footprint against a different baseline.
  • Eaton's CEO states Boyd is "the market leaders for liquid cooling, if you think about the cold plates and the CDUs." TrendForce names four cold-plate suppliers — Cooler Master, AVC, Boyd and Auras — and reports that sidecar coolant distribution units, the larger half of that market, are led by Delta, with in-row units supplied primarily by Vertiv and Boyd
  • eMobility was launched in 2018 as the vehicle to scale power electronics into mobile markets, targeted at $1.2bn of 2025 revenue at 11% margin in March 2022 with the bar marked "Booked ~87%", raised to $1.5bn in October 2023, and delivered $604m with a $14m operating loss.
value-chain
  • The equipment layer where Eaton operates captures about 5% of each data-centre dollar and the compute layer captures most of the rest.
  • For the next four to six quarters it is inside Eaton's factories: firm backlog reached $24.1bn at 30 June 2026 from $19.8bn at 31 December 2025, 71% of it targeted for delivery inside twelve months, with Electrical Americas book-to-bill at 1.3 and trailing organic orders up 41%.
  • Over the twelve months to June 2026 WESCO's gross margin rose to 21.8% from 21.1% while Eaton's consolidated gross margin declined to 33.5% from 37.0%. The distributor passed the commodity increase through and kept some.
  • Each of those reduces the physical content per megawatt of the layer Eaton sells, while the CEO states content per megawatt holds at $3.4m through the transition. Eaton's case has to be that it wins a larger share of a smaller physical bill of materials, or that solid-state transformers and cooling add more value than the conversion stages remove.

The FY2025 10-K states that sales recognised over time were approximately 7% of consolidated net sales in 2025 and under 5% in 2024 and 2023, against Systems at $13,000m or 47% of consolidated sales.

business-explainer · business-model · ↩ back to dossier

Company

filingqualifies
In the Aerospace segment, sales contracts are primarily for aerospace fuel, hydraulics, and pneumatic systems for commercial and military use, as well as filtration systems for industrial applications. These sales contracts are primarily based on a customer’s purchase order, and frequently covered by terms and conditions included in a long-term agreement. In this segment, performance obligations are generally satisfied at a point in time either when we ship the product from our facility, or when it arrives at the customer’s facility. Our military contracts are primarily fixed-price contracts that are not subject to performance-based payments or progress payments from the customer.
— Eaton Corporation plc Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-24
filingsupports
The operating margin increased from 23.0% in 2024 to 23.9% in 2025. Material factors affecting this increase were a 540 basis point increase from higher sales, partially offset by a 260 basis point decline from higher commodity and wage inflation, a 70 basis point decline from operating inefficiencies, a 60 basis point decline from higher costs to support growth initiatives, and a 60 basis point decline from the sale of a production facility in the first quarter of 2024.
— Eaton Corporation plc Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-24

Peer: TransDigm Group Incorporated

filingsupports
Most of our products generate significant aftermarket revenue. Once our parts are designed into and sold on a new aircraft, we generate net sales from aftermarket consumption over the life of that aircraft, which is generally estimated to be approximately 25 to 30 years. A typical platform can be produced for 20 to 30 years, giving us an estimated product life cycle in excess of 50 years. We estimate that approximately 55% of our net sales in fiscal year 2025 were generated from the aftermarket, the vast majority of which come from the commercial and military aftermarkets. Historically, these aftermarket revenues have produced a higher gross profit and have been more stable than net sales to original equipment manufacturers.
— TransDigm Group Incorporated Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2025-11-13
filingcontradicts
As is customary in our business, we do not generally have long-term contracts with most of our aftermarket customers and, therefore, do not have guaranteed future sales. Although we have long-term contracts with many of our OEM customers, many of those customers may terminate the contracts on short notice and, in most cases, our customers have not committed to buy any minimum quantity of our products.
— TransDigm Group Incorporated Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2025-11-13

filingsupports ✦ best
The Company is exposed to fluctuations in commodity prices due to volatility in raw material costs and contractual agreements with suppliers. To partially mitigate this exposure, Eaton enters into commodity contracts for certain raw material purchases with the objective of minimizing changes in inventory cost due to market price fluctuations. These commodity contracts are designated for hedge accounting and are generally less than one year in duration. Based on Eaton’s best estimate for a hypothetical 10% fluctuation in commodity prices the gain or loss would be less than $1 million. The sensitivity analysis of the effects of changes in commodity prices assumes the notional value to remain constant for the next 12 months.
— Eaton Corporation plc Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-24

On March 12, 2026, Eaton acquired Boyd Thermal for $9.55 billion, net of cash acquired. Boyd Thermal is a U.S. based global leader in thermal components, systems, and ruggedized solutions for data center, aerospace and other end-markets. Boyd Thermal employs more than 6,000 people with manufacturing sites across North America, Asia, and Europe. Boyd Thermal is reported within the Electrical Global business segment.
— Eaton Corporation plc Form 10-Q for the quarter ended June 30, 2026, 2026-07-31

Against Allison Transmission, which converts a comparable commercial-vehicle drivetrain incumbency into a 33.1% EBITDA margin and roughly 21% ROIC, Mobility runs 16 points behind on margin and roughly 9–12 points behind on returns.

A solid-state transformer replaces the wound transformer with a power-electronics stage that converts the utility feed straight to DC, so the DC flows to the rack with no further conversions.

business-explainer · technology · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callsupports
Now moving to Slide 6. We highlight our most recent acquisition, Resilient Power Systems. This is a great example of how Eaton is investing for growth through cutting-edge innovation. This is a game changer for our data center customers and other DC power applications. Resilient makes solid-state transformer technology to replace traditional copper windings. It is a critical building block in the future high-power AI center designs as well as EV charging and battery storage.
— Paulo Ruiz Sternadt, CEO & Director, Eaton Corporation plc, Q2 2025 Earnings Call, 2025-08-05
earnings callsupports
And Eaton is extremely well-positioned for this change, not only because today our products touch every conversion of AC and DC in the data center, but also because we have decade-long experience with dealing with DC power in other segments, like machinery, industrial facilities and also eMobility, we're dealing with DC power for a while.
— Paulo Ruiz Sternadt, CEO & Director, Eaton Corporation plc, Q3 2025 Earnings Call, 2025-11-04
earnings callsupports
If you look at today's efficiency, even in the most improved designs in AC, efficiency runs at 93%. And we estimate and all the industry leaders estimate that switching to this direct current technology in 800 volts or above can save up to 5% from data center operations, moving the efficiency all the way up to 98%. So if you think about this, this is huge dollars and huge efficiency gains that can change completely the economics of the data center.
— Paulo Ruiz Sternadt, CEO & Director, Eaton Corporation plc, Q1 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-05-05
earnings callsupports
The fact that we acquired Resilient Power Systems accelerated our tech development because we acquired an immersion cooled offering that drives much more power density in a much smaller footprint. So it really leapfrogged our evolution here. And we have more than a handful of solid-state transformer pilots -- actually approaching two handful, including hyperscaler customers.
— Paulo Ruiz Sternadt, CEO & Director, Eaton Corporation plc, Q1 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-05-05
presentationsupports ✦ best
So -- and when you look at power coming into a data center, if we start there, there's a utility feed and that's medium voltage, and then it goes through a traditional transform, gets stepped down to low voltage and gets distributed throughout the data center, and all that's AC power until it gets to the rack. The medium voltage solid-state transformer takes the utility feed and can convert it directly using power electronics into DC, direct current. That direct current can then flow throughout the data center through all the switchgear and all the bus bar directly into the rack. So you're avoiding all the conversions. You're already at DC power, which is what the servers and the chips take in order to provide the electrical power to make them run.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17
presentationsupports
So that's why that's so important. It eliminates the loss, but it simplifies the whole architecture of the data center. Everything now is direct current.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17
presentationqualifies
And there's one other thing that we're really looking at when we're helping customers plan out these architectures, and that's the circuit protection and power distribution side of this. It's one thing to be able to convert it, but you're talking about now a lot of power into a very small area with highly valuable equipment. And the circuit protection world has been designed around alternating current. And so now you have to come out with a whole new class of protective devices to be able to make sure that you can circumvent fault conditions.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17
presentationqualifies
When you have alternating current, the current goes up and down below 0, and where 0 voltages, that's where the actual circuit breaker and the mechanical device can interrupt the circuit safely. When you have direct current, the voltage level is always on. So you have to interrupt the circuit at a much faster pace if you're going to provide a safe condition.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17
presentationsupports
And if you just look at from the utility feed all the way through to the rack, and you step down voltages going from medium voltage to low voltage and then down to 54 volts into the rack and the conversion from AC to DC and back, we estimate that there's roughly about 5% electrical loss during that transition. If you could just go from DC directly from the utility feed all the way through to data center into the rack, that's 5% efficiency gain that you could get.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17
presentationqualifies
Can people get their all in one shot? No. So as you mentioned, Julian, there're some bridge strategies to get there with 400 volts. But the underlying movement towards the direct recurrent, that's pervasive and that's going to happen.
— Michael Regelski, CTO, Electrical Sector, Eaton Corporation plc presents at Barclays 43rd Annual Industrial Select Conference, 2026-02-17

Why cold plates are important. They are a mirrored image of a chip. So every chip has a different heat map, and you cannot swap a cold plate from one chip to another. It's closer to what we do in businesses like aerospace or even mobility markets where you win a position in a platform and you start getting the orders.

Each of those reduces the physical content per megawatt of the layer Eaton sells, while the CEO states content per megawatt holds at $3.4m through the transition. Eaton's case has to be that it wins a larger share of a smaller physical bill of materials, or that solid-state transformers and cooling add more value than the conversion stages remove.

With 800 VDC, the same wire gauge can carry 157% more power than 415 VAC. Using a simpler three-wire setup (POS, RTN, PE) instead of four for AC, fewer conductors and smaller connectors are required. This reduces copper use, lowers material and installation costs.

websupports
A native DC architecture eliminates multiple, inefficient AC-to-DC conversion steps that occur in traditional systems, where end-to-end efficiency can be less than 90%. A DC distribution system is inherently simpler, with fewer components like transformers and phase-balancing equipment. This reduction in complexity leads to fewer potential points of failure and increases overall system reliability.
— NVIDIA Technical Blog, 'Building the 800 VDC Ecosystem for Efficient, Scalable AI Factories', 2025-10-13
